===== [p237-237] Alignment > 8 micron error: (1块) =====
--[p237]-- If for some reason the mark signal is deformed (e.g. the 8 µm and 8.8 µm gratings are shifted with respect to each other) or the mark is rotated the software may decide that the “best position” is one 8 µm maximum further than it should. This results in an 8 micron error.
This error is not visible on the monitor. Since the 8.0 µm signal is the “holy ruler” 8.8 µm errors don’t occur.

===== [p235-236] Alignment > 88 micron error: (3块) =====
--[p235]-- An overlapping positive top of both sine waves will repeat every 88 micron. This results in a capture range of +/- 44 µm.
If the mark is more then 44 µm shifted with respect to the expected position the software will find a “best” aligned position 88 µm further on. (11 x 8.0 = 10 x 8.8)
This is an “88 micron” error and is visible on the monitor on the operator console.
This error can occur in all four directions (+/- X and +/- Y).
The top picture shows correctly aligned marks.
--[p236]-- The top picture shows correctly aligned marks.
The picture in the middle shows an 88 micron error in x direction.
The bottom picture shows two alignment errors (x and y)
This type of error will be visible on the alignment monitor on the operator console during the fine phase of an alignment scan.
During coarse phase of the alignment this error will not be visible since “wrong peak” detection takes place after the actual coarse scan has been performed i.e. during modeling of the collected data from the detectors.
--[p236]-- This means that only during the fine phase of an alignment scenario this type of error shows up.

===== [p183-183] LEVEL SENSOR (LS) FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > ASML CONFIDENTIAL (2块) =====
--[p183]-- In total there are 6 different possibilities as described on the slide. Theoretically it would also be possible that the level sensor functionality in a field changes from None\_By\_LS to Z\_By\_LS. But the software will NOT allow this to happen. The level sensor functionality can never increase. That means that the scan direction for a lot of fields is fixed. Especially edge fields are mostly scanned in-out.
--[p183]-- Especially edge fields are mostly scanned in-out.
This means that they are scanned from the inner part of the wafer (more valid white spots) to the outer part of the wafer (less valid white spots). This can have a very big negative impact on throughput.
If we take a closer look at the total scan, we see that we have to take into account the pre-scan and the slit scan length in determination of the leveling mode. If a field needs to be leveled, than the pre- and slit scan need to be leveled with the same or a higher number of valid spots.
This means that if this field is on the edge of the wafer it can only be scanned in-out.

===== [p18-18] Wavelength vs. Depth of Focus (2) >  Lens Aperture (2块) =====
--[p18]--  The projection lens aperture defines the diameter of the lens at the pupil. On /40, /50 and /60 steppers the aperture is set to the maximum lens diameter and is not adjustable.
 On /100, /200, /300… the aperture can be adjusted to a setting smaller than the maximum size of the lens.
 The numeric aperture setting determines the size of the lens aperture.
 The adjustable aperture is an electrically driven, mechanical aperture that is controlled by PAS software.
--[p18]-- Optical Theory 48142 v00, 12-07-2000 14
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
For example, on a /550 the NA is adjustable between values of 0.4 and 0.63. For a /700 the NA is adjustable between values of 0.5 and 0.7. The new (/700) lens is able to resolve a period (repeating line/space) of 0.360-micron, compared with the previous lens’ ability to resolve a period of 0.400-micron (using conventional illumination). See next page.

===== [p240-240] Alignment > Alignment parameters: (2块) =====
--[p240]-- This is not checked.
The delta 8.0 to 8.8 micron shift is checked as well.
--[p240]-- MCC = Multiple Correlation Coefficient, the software fits a sine wave to the alignment signal, the quality of the fit is called MCC, where a value of 1 stands for a perfect sine, 0 for a very bad sine.
A MCC value of => 0.7 is the minimum. This is used as a validation criterion. Normally values are higher than 0.9d as a validation criterion.
The Wafer Quality values reflect the amount of signal coming directly from the scanned mark. This is not checked.

===== [p182-183] LEVEL SENSOR (LS) FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Different Leveling Modes (4块) =====
--[p182]-- For height and tilt we have to define whether a field can be measured completely, partially or not at all.
 Inner fields (measured completely)
 Intra fields (measured partially)
 Inter fields (not measured)
Distinguishing between types of fields on the wafer is important to understanding how the leveling system models the wafer. To distinguish between different fields on the wafer, we have to specify which parameter can be measured completely, partially or not at all.
--[p183 [表]]-- Level sensor functionality | Height | Tilt
All_By_LS to All_By_LS | Inner | Inner
All_By_LS to Z_By_LS | Inner | Intra
All_By_LS to None_By_LS | Intra | Intra
Z_By_LS to Z_By_LS | Inner | Inter
Z_By_LS to None_By_LS | Intra | Inter
None_By_LS to None_By_LS | Inter | Inter
Level sensor 48142 v00, 25/04/00 20
--[p182]-- This means the reference information is taken from within the field and we call this field intra for tilt. So for every field we have a separate indication for both height and tilt.
--[p182]-- For example look at a field that starts to be exposed in All\_By\_LS and during the exposure changes to Z\_By\_LS (previous page). The height for this cell can be measured completely, no reference information is needed. That means that for height this field is called an inner field. The tilt for this field can only be measured partially (in the All\_By\_LS part of the field). In the Z\_By\_LS part we need reference information and this can be taken from the All\_By\_LS part of the same field.

===== [p262-262] Hardware overview > Example (4块) =====
--[p262]-- Now the level scans can be queued as well as the alignment scans.
This also makes it possible to make concatenated alignment scans (WS doesn’t stop in between scans).
--[p262]-- TTL would align these marks by performing an alignment on the first mark (leveling is done during the movement to the mark), then a leveling measurement is done close to the second mark followed by an alignment action. This is followed by a similar action for mark three. Mark four only requires an alignment action (with the leveling done in parallel). The fifth mark needs a separate level action again.
--[p262]-- The fifth mark needs a separate level action again.
The software modules LC (leveling Control) and CD (CoorDination control) are used alternatingly.
In the case of FTTL first all level information that cannot be obtained during the alignment scan is gathered, so three level scans are made (for the NoneByLS markers) followed by 5 alignment actions (the other two marks are leveled during the scan). Now the level scans can be queued as well as the alignment scans.
--[p262]-- Whereas an alignment is done sequentially in the case of TTL, the alignment with FTTL is arranged such that the actions are queued and performed parallel .
Example: 5 markers have to be aligned. These markers are distributed over the wafer, such that the first and fourth can be leveled Z\_by\_LS and the others are in a None\_by\_LS area.

===== [p127-128] Stage Movement Design > Scanning Wafer Stage Leveling (4块) =====
--[p127]-- Three level actuators (cam disks) move the mirror block in z, Rx and Ry.
 Three LVDTs measure mirror block height with respect to the airfoot.
Levelling of the wafer stage is necessary to make sure the wafer surface is in the focal plane of the lens during exposure. Where possible the three level actuators are under the control of the level sensor. The level sensor measures the distance between the wafer surface and the projection lens.
--[p127]-- The level sensor measures the distance between the wafer surface and the projection lens.
The LVDTs measure the height of the mirror block above the airfoot. Level sensor and LVDT data are stored for different stage positions. Thus when, due to stage position, the level sensor is unable to function, LVDT data from a neighboring stage location, where the level sensor was effective, are used as reference for leveling. The level sensor is generally not effective close to the edge of the wafer. More information regarding leveling can be found in the level sensor presentation.
--[p128]-- This array is located between two circular extraction channels.
Thus the air flows both towards the center and the edge of the airfoot. The cavity in the center of the airfoot is evacuated.
The combination between air pressure and vacuum serves to create a very stable air bearing between the airfoot and the granite stone. The gap is approximately 15 µm and is maintained very accurately. An analogous system is used to facilitate reticle stage movement.
--[p127]-- More information regarding leveling can be found in the level sensor presentation.
Absolute leveling data (position in Z and tilt in X,Y) for each field is calculated from the LVDT data and included in the full batch report.
A schematic cross-section and a photograph of the bottom of an airfoot are shown in the slide above.
An air bearing is used to make the airfoot float over the stone. This comprises a flow of high pressure air directed through a circular array of holes in the bottom of the airfoot. This array is located between two circular extraction channels.

===== [p353-353] CONTAMINATION & TEMPERATURE CONTROL (C&T) >  Typical lack of cooling capacity situation (2块) =====
--[p353]-- ASML CONFIDENTIAL
• During exposure unit operation, the heat load to the TCU fluctuates. The TCU aims at preventing fluctuations in water temperature.
• If the TCU cooling capacity is insufficient however, the system will warm-up slowly, resulting in an increase in lens temperature.
• An increase in the lens temperature results in a decreasing TCU set point. If the TCU lacks capacity, the TCU set point drifts away from the water temperature.
--[p353]-- If the TCU lacks capacity, the TCU set point drifts away from the water temperature.
• When the difference between the water temperature and the TCU set point is above 2 °C the C&T driver in the PAS software terminates.

===== [p124-124] Stage Movement Design > Reticle Chuck Leveling (4块) =====
--[p124]-- Rather three cam disks are positioned under the granite stone over which the reticle chuck floats.
Using these level actuators, height and tilt of the reticle stage stone and therefore the reticle chuck are continually modified while scanning.
The height and tilt of the reticle chuck is continuously measured by four capacitive sensors. Four gold strips underneath the reticle stage and four sensor plates on the top of the lens form four measurement pairs. The layout of these sensors was designed to have at least three measurement points no matter where the stage is located.
--[p124]-- Details of the lens top-view:
The corner cubes in the lens top view are the corner cubes for the Y-interferometer beams that are aimed at the top edge of the lens. The x-mirror is for the x-interferometer beams aimed at the top edge of the lens. The two accelerometers detect movements of the lens top (one in x, one in y). The purpose of these components will be explained in the following slides. The quad cells are for the reticle fine pre-alignment.
--[p124]-- The quad cells are for the reticle fine pre-alignment.
This will be explained in the section on stage interface design.
--[p124]-- Real time, on the fly reticle stage leveling, referenced to the lens
The reticle does not need to be leveled with the same accuracy as the wafer. A 1 µm change in reticle height causes a 1/16 µm focus variation at wafer level (the square of the lens magnification).
The reticle is not independently leveled. Rather three cam disks are positioned under the granite stone over which the reticle chuck floats.

===== [p227-227] Alignment > Stage to Stage Alignment (Skew) (4块) =====
--[p227]-- The distance in the x-direction is a measurement of the lens magnification.
In order to expose the correct Y-size of the image the found magnification will be used to calculate what the nominal distance from G1 to H1 should be for a correct “scan scale”. Two alignment scans are involved: F2 - G1 (M1) is scanned to measure the position of G1. Without moving the reticle stage: F2 - G2 (M2), G2 is measured on F2, the wafer stage has to move F2 under alignment branch M2. This is under control of the x and y waferstage interferometer system.
--[p227]-- This way, the wafer stage is forced to exactly follow the path of the aerial image, so that the image is not distorted. Finally, the H1, G1 and G2 positions in the waferstage coordinates also determines the translation of the reticle stages grid versus the wafer stage grid, until a reticle alignment updates the reticle stage grid.
--[p227]-- This is under control of the x and y waferstage interferometer system.
Any difference between expected x-position (G2) and actual found x-position of G2 is due to lens magnification. Any difference between expected y-position of G2 and found yposition is the angle between the waferstage grid and the reticle stage grid, the skew.
When during the stage to stage alignment a scan skew is measured, it is compensated by changing the x-values of the waferstage start and end positions for each scan.
--[p227]--  F2 - G1 (Branch M1)
F2 - G2 (Branch M2)
Scan skew: is defined as the rotation of the reticle stage coordinate system with respect to the wafer coordinate system. Measurement of G1 and G2 provides information about the reticle stage rotation (skew) from their found position in the y-direction compared to the expected position. The distance in the x-direction is a measurement of the lens magnification.

===== [p221-221] Alignment > Coarse Align: (1块) =====
--[p221]-- In the coarse alignment phase, the wafer or fiducial marks are scanned diagonally under the reticle marks. This gives x and y information in one scan, any wafer or reticle rotation will be corrected after the coarse alignment.
For the wafer coarse align only two marks are used regardless of how many marks are available.
The marks must be >20 mm apart - this value cannot be changed. They must have a capture range of 88 µm i.e. with 8.0 and 8.8 micron gratings.

===== [p267-268] Using Off Axis alignment in production > Influence of mark depth: two wavelengths are used (3块) =====
--[p268]-- The first order is used to indicate the which maximum of the higher orders is the best aligned position for that order. See picture.
If the first order is not present, the expected position is used to indicate which maximum of the third orders should be chosen. It’s advised to make sure that there are always at least two first order marks present. E.g. XPA’s or PM’s.
--[p267]-- • Although 120 nm is the ‘advised’ mark depth, sometimes marks are etched deeper.
Example: a zero-first combined mark. That’s why the graphs show a large range of mark depths.
To keep a capture range of ±44  m the first order of the 8.0  m grating and the 8.8  m grating are used during the coarse phase, the same way as in TTL alignment. (No higher orders from the 8.8  m grating are present.)
The 8.8  m signal is used to indicate which maximum from the 8.0  m first order is the correct one.
--[p267]-- This slide shows how using two wavelengths (green, 532 nm and red, 633 nm) reduces the sensitivity to mark depth.
(Mark depth can decrease due to processing.)
The slide shows:
• Swing curve for one wavelength: sometimes no signal!
•The two colours have different swing curves
• Combine the two, ‘switch between curves’: always a signal to align on.
So less influence from mark-depth deviations.
• Although 120 nm is the ‘advised’ mark depth, sometimes marks are etched deeper.

– Initial orientation of coordinate system.

X axis is defined by the average direction of both Xinterferometer beams.
Y axis is defined by the average direction of both Yinterferometer beams.

===== [p498-499] Dose Control FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Method: (4块) =====
--[p499]-- -The tilt is then measured by the LS ( using white system, AllbyLS )
In principle, the LS height & tilt is measured during metro setup, so there is no longer any need to measure stone to lens tilt using FEM jobs.
--[p499]-- The offsets for both tilts are stored as machine constants (MC)
measure the values for the other plane. i.e measure the relative tilt between the two. e.g.
To measure Stone to Lens tilt
\- The LS height and tilt is already known, and stored in MC
-The wafer table is adjusted to the MC values for Stone to Lens tilt. ( using red system, ZbyLS )
During initial setup, this would be 0
-The tilt is then measured by the LS ( using white system, AllbyLS )
between the LS plate and the stone
--[p498]-- Note: there is no red system height measurement in each die after white level. This is because the slit area is small, so wafer unflatness has little effect.
Stone to lens tilt versus Level Sensor Tilt
Purpose: Fast calibration of stone to lens tilt.
( If level sensor tilt is known, it is possible to calculate the stone to lens tilt - and vice versa.)
The offsets for both tilts are stored as machine constants (MC)
If one of these MC’s is known, then that plane can be used as a reference to
--[p498]-- -The wafer is globally levelled (red and white)
-The first die is white levelled (allbyLS)
This brings the wafer surface level with the level sensor plate
-The die matrix is exposed, stepping the wafer in Z
-The sequence is then repeated for the other two dies.
The best focus of each position in each die is then read under the microscope, and the tilt calculated for each die. The average of the 3 tilt measurements is then used to update the MC’s

===== [p484-486] Dose Control FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Uniformity (stepper test) (3块) =====
--[p484]-- For reference,
Here we see the results of a uniformity check from the stepper.
This shows the uniformity over the whole lens field.
In the scanner, this is no longer a valid check, because we only use the slit area during exposure.
Therefore, we need a different way of measuring uniformity.
Integrated Slit Uniformity
The Integrated Slit Uniformity check uses the spot sensor to measure a grid of 11 lines in the X direction, with each line comprising 51 measurement positions in Y.
--[p486]-- If we used dynamic mode, the readings would be affected by pulse power variations from the laser, and velocity variations of the stages.
Dose Contro 48142 v00, 16-03-2001 10
--[p486]-- At each position, 100 pulses of the laser are fired, to minimize the effect of any pulse energy variations.
In each line, the measurements from the 51 positions are integrated, to give an average intensity for the line.
The integrated value from each of the 11 lines is then used to calculate the slit uniformity.
Note that this test is done in static mode.
The reason for this is that we want to measure the uniformity of the lens only, as this is a static, repeatable effect.

===== [p358-358] CONTAMINATION & TEMPERATURE CONTROL (C&T) >  Air Flows (2块) =====
--[p358]-- The fan for air extraction is positioned in the S&T cabinet.
The fan creates an under-pressure and the air is supplied from the clean air in the environment.
In scanners, air is also extracted from the reticle stage (R-pins). The extraction system also removes air from the velocity drive box to prevent its heated air disturbing the interferometer-bundles.
Because the RS-LIMMS and WS-LIMMS motors have air bearings instead of mechanical bearings, air extraction is not needed.
--[p358]-- • Extraction Air system : In several subsystems or components contamination is expected and likely to occur. Particles are expected where mechanical movements take place causing rubbing. These subsystems are separated from the environment using enclosures, from which air is extracted.
A fan extracts the contaminated air, which is extracted via a duct. The fan for air extraction is positioned in the S&T cabinet.

===== [p290-294] TRANSMISSION IMAGE SENSOR (TIS) (5块) =====
--[p290]-- FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY
Transmission Image Sensor 48142 v00, 27/04/00 1
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
1st version: 01-11-1999
2nd version: 11-11-1999
3th version: 05-04-2000
4th version 27-04-2000
This presentation introduces the Transmission Image Sensor (TIS) and compares it with its predecessor, the Reflection Image Sensor (RIS).
--[p293]-- As was discussed in the RIS module, an image sensor can be used to monitor and correct stepper and scanner X, Y and Z positioning drifts during operation, as an aid to enhancing productivity.
The Image Sensor used on systems without ATHENA is called the Reflection Image Sensor (RIS). However, because the new Advanced Alignment system (ATHENA) has been developed, there was a requirement to develop a new type of Image Sensor, called the Transmission Image Sensor (TIS).
--[p294]-- Therefore, if ATHENA is implemented, we cannot use RIS.
The TIS system, however, is located in the wafer stage, and therefore can be used together with ATHENA.
Improved performance will be obtained using TIS instead of the RIS system. The improvement is that the Image Sensor (IS) measurements do not have to be made at fixed positions in the blue image (exposure) field (as they were with RIS).
--[p294]-- RIS scans are made by measuring the positions of three marks in the image field simultaneously, and calculating the system drifts from this information. The TIS system, however, measures only one mark at a time. This makes it also possible (in theory at least) to expose the same mark at different y-positions in the image field and measure it on wafer level (not done (yet)).
--[p293]-- ATHENA cannot be used in combination with the RIS system because the new alignment system hardware is positioned under the sensor plate, in a location also required by RIS hardware.
TIS is introduced as the new Image Sensor for the following reasons:
ASML has developed a new advanced alignment system called ATHENA.
To incorporate ATHENA on the PAS5500 requires the placement of an optical module under the sensor plate, in an area reserved for one set of RIS detectors.

===== [p210-210] Alignment > Aligned position: (1块) =====
--[p210]-- Wafer mark to reticle mark alignment is achieved when the wafer stage position is found, at which the image of the wafer mark exactly overlays the reticle mark. This is called the aligned position. Actually, the wafer stage does not necessarily move to that position; instead, the position is calculated from the measurement results. You can see alignment being performed by looking at the alignment monitor on the operator console.

===== [p467-468] Stage Synchronization Accuracy: an Analogy (2块) =====
--[p467]-- Scanning stages must be synchronized or image problems will result Accuracy required is same as 2 aircraft flying only 10 microns apart at 1000 KPH.
(1000 km/hr = 360 m/s. Divide by 360 to get to 1 m/s. Doing the same for 10 microns yields a dynamic overlay of approximately 28 nm. Which is the correct order of magnitude.)
--[p468]-- Doing the same for 10 microns yields a dynamic overlay of approximately 28 nm.

===== [p64-64] Machine Access FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Machine Access (3块) =====
--[p64]-- ASML machines are easy to service due to modular design.
 All covers can be removed:
– Pushing the rubber tip of the latch tool through an access hole releases the cover lock.
Parts of the machine can be swung out (or moved) to give
Latch tool
access to selected parts of the machine.
– Wafer stage, REMA objective, electronics rack, ARMS, Wafer Handler
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
‘Modular design’ means that the Step & Scan systems are built-up from separate units (modules).
--[p64]-- ‘Modular design’ means that the Step & Scan systems are built-up from separate units (modules).
The modules are individually assembled and calibrated at the factory, then put together for testing. After performing within specification, the system is taken apart to be shipped. At the customer site the modules are put together once again.
In case of system defects, errors are usually confined to single modules. Having a modular design thus eases the search for possible causes of errors, speeds up repairs and minimizes ‘down-time’.
--[p64]-- Also, in case of persisting defects, entire modules can be replaced so as to minimize the ‘down-time’ of the system.
Service procedures provide detailed information on how to remove and re-attach covers. Service procedures also exist on how to swing out (and swing back in) specific parts of the machine. Note that swinging out parts of the machine may require specific covers to be removed first.

===== [p207-207] Alignment > Wafer pre-alignment: (2块) =====
--[p207]-- If necessary, the wafer is re-centred and re-measured.
Modelling of the data allows the flat or notch to be found and hence the orientation of the wafer.
After the wafer has been edge pre-aligned, it may be optically pre-aligned using the mark sensor.
This uses a CCD camera and pattern recognition of the marks.
The accuracy of wafer pre-alignment is app.. 3 µm.
--[p207]-- Wafer pre-alignment is done on the P-chuck.
The wafer is always pre-aligned first with the edge sensor which works by detecting the amount of light blocked by the wafer as it rotates.
If the centre of rotation does not correspond to the wafer centre the signal from the CCD chip will vary as the wafer rotates.
This allows the eccentricity and hence the centre of the wafer to be measured.
If necessary, the wafer is re-centred and re-measured.

===== [p275-278] REFLECTION IMAGE SENSOR (RIS) > FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY (2块) =====
--[p278]-- The aerial image is defined as the image of the pattern at reticle level, which is projected by the lens to a point below the lens, which defines the focal plane.
The image sensor can measure these values without the need for exposing wafers and reading test exposures. It provides an ideal means for automatic calibration of the step and scan system.
--[p275]-- ASML CONFIDENTIAL
1st version: 08-11-1999
2nd version: 12-11-1999
3th version: 24-11-1999
4th version 03-04-2000
5th version 27-04-2000
The Image Sensor is a calibration tool designed to keep the projection properties (the Image Quality Parameters) of a PAS 5500 step and scan system calibrated.
The Reflection Image Sensor - RIS- directly measures the change (drift) in position, size, rotation, height, and tilt of the aerial image.

===== [p270-270] Using Off Axis alignment in production > • Three general types of recipes: (2块) =====
--[p270]-- Predictive average: for certain processes there is a relationship between the aligned positions of the different orders. Using the relationship the best aligned position is calculated. (Also not used, needs further investigation.)
• There are of course many variations on these three types! It’s beyond the scope of this course to discuss them in detail.
--[p270]-- Static average: from experience it is determined for a certain process which orders should be used and with what weight factor (WF).
Example: see next slide
Dynamic average: based on wafer quality or MCC a choice of the measured signals is made and/or weight factors are ‘assigned’.
Since there is no evidence at the moment that this works better then the static type, it is not used.

===== [p123-123] Stage Movement Design > Reticle Chuck Layout (Bottom View) (2块) =====
--[p123]-- Through a hole in the granite stone, vacuum is applied to the vacuum channels. By balancing the air pressure with the vacuum, a stable air bearing is created between stage and stone. The thickness of the bearing is kept to a value between 9 and 13 µm. Note that the range of stage movement is limited by the length of the vacuum channels.
A similar principle is used to facilitate wafer stage movement.
--[p123]-- The slide above shows a schematic of the bottom of the reticle chuck.
The gold strips make up part of the capacitive z sensors used for reticle chuck height measurements (explained later).
To maintain a stable air barrier between reticle chuck and the reticle stage stone, a system of pressure and vacuum is used. The holes supply air pressure. At the small channels the air is extracted to make sure the (heated) air does not interfere with the lens, capacitive sensors or surroundings.

===== [p354-354] CONTAMINATION & TEMPERATURE CONTROL (C&T) > Water System (3) (1块) =====
--[p354]--  Water Flows
• In Scanner systems, the primary heat exchanger is a standard item and a connection is provided from the manifolds.
• Newly designed airmounts for scanners also need water cooling.
• In the stages (RS & WS), a short stroke motor (SSM) and a long stroke motor (LSM) are used. The connection between these two in the wafer stage - velocity drive box - also needs cooling with cooling water.

===== [p136-136] Stage Movement Design > Wafer Stage Initialization (3块) =====
--[p136]-- Firstly the short stroke motors are centered on the difference sensor. Then the long stroke motors move the stage to its home position at the front left of the machine. The stage rotation is then coarsely determined by the Y interferometer beams. Next the stage moves to the zero sensors (arrays of magnets on the stage and hall sensors on the sensor plate). The distance the stage moves is defined in the machine constants. Here the origin of the coordinate system is defined.
--[p136]-- Here the origin of the coordinate system is defined.
The precision with which the coordinate system is determined using the zero sensors is of the order of a few microns. This is sufficient to ensure marks can be brought within the capture range of the alignment system. The stage then moves by a predefined distance in X to a third Hall sensor where residual stage rotation is corrected.
--[p136]-- – Determination of the initial wafer stage coordinate system.
– Orientation of coordinate system (determined by measurement hardware).
 Z axis is determined by the level sensor (calibrated by test)
 X axis is defined by the average direction of the x-interferometer beams.
 Y axis is defined by the average direction of the y-interferometer beams.
– Zero coordinate system:
During initialization the wafer stage coordinate system in which the stage moves prior to alignment is determined.

===== [p161-161] Stage Movement Design > Middle: (2块) =====
--[p161]-- By default, a reticle align is performed each time a reticle is loaded.
Note that the rotation will be measured with respect to the WSCS!
--[p161]-- We do not scan the reticle by itself, however, we place it on a reticle stage. While doing so, we will make a rotation and translation error. E.g. the reticle will be rotated with respect to the reticle stage. Since the stage is going to move the reticle, it is essential that we know how much this rotation and translation is. The reticle rotation is measured during reticle align. By default, a reticle align is performed each time a reticle is loaded.

===== [p350-350] CONTAMINATION & TEMPERATURE CONTROL (C&T) >  C&T Log file (3块) =====
--[p350]-- The setpoints that were calculated by the PAS software are also in the C&T logfile.

===== [p228-228] Alignment > Summary of Stage to Stage Alignment: (1块) =====
--[p228]-- Following fine alignment scans will be performed:
\- F2-H1 (Branch M1)
\- F2-G1 (Branch M1)
\- F2-G2 (Branch M2)
From these scans the following wafer parameters are determined:
\- RS translation X,Y (with respect to WSCS)
\- Scan scaling
\- Scan skew (Reticle stage rotation)
Measured errors will be corrected during exposure:
\- Adjust stage movement x and y
\- Redefine start & end x and y-positions of wafer stage

===== [p222-222] Alignment > Alignment before wafer exposure: (1块) =====
--[p222]-- In principle there are two possibilities:
When the wafer contains no marks yet, exposure is named zerolayer exposure. Wafer positioning is based on pre-alignment only.
When the wafer has a number (at least two) of marks this is called a non zerolayer exposure.
This has some consequences for alignment, see next slides.

===== [p62-62] Machine Access FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Machine Covers (2块) =====
--[p62]-- Covers are present on the ASML Step & Scan systems to:
– Shield moving parts that are potentially dangerous to personnel.

– Create a clean and stable working environment inside the machine. (E.g. maintain constant pressure and temperature.)

===== [p336-336] O > Projection Lens /500 (4块) =====
--[p336]--  0.40 - 0.63 NA, 0.22  m imaging, extendable to 0.18  m
 Increased field size, not limited by lens diameter (in scan direction)
 Lens aberrations are reduced because of the smaller (slit) field
 Lens aberrations are averaged over the slit width in scan direction
 On-the-fly leveling gives real time tilt correction in scan direction
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
The projection lens is type 79, dual telecentric, 4X reduction [lens].
Why 4x reduction?
-Reticle size is limited to 6 inch.
--[p336]-- nts are rotated to put the areas of least distortion inside the slit exposure area
•Imaging performance is a dynamic process rather than a static process.
• Field size is - in the scanning direction - not limited by the lens diameter anymore, but rather by the scan range provided by the stages, and by the reticle size.
• All lens aberrations (astigmatism, distortion, etc.) are reduced because the effective field of the lens (the projection slit) is smaller, which makes it easier to control aberrations, and
--[p336]-- -Reticle size is limited to 6 inch.
For large dies, the reticle size became the limiting factor, thus the decision was made to increase lifetime of the 6-inch reticle format by changing the reduction factor of the projection lens.
Type 79 lens is the same as type 78 (/300), except that the individual lens elements are rotated to put the areas of least distortion inside the slit exposure area
Because the /500 is a scanning system, it has fundamental differences from the steppers , which are summarized below:
--[p336]-- • Aberrations (such as distortion etc.) are averaged (over the slit width) in scan direction
• On-the-fly leveling provides real time tilt correction in the scan direction.

===== [p424-424] Job definition > 2nd order mirror curvature: (1块) =====

===== [p330-330] O > The quartz rod (1块) =====
--[p330]-- There are two quartz rods in the assembly, one installed vertically and the other one installed horizontally. The light that enters the quartz rod is reflected approximately 900 times to homogenise the light intensity. The result is a uniform light distribution.
A mirror at a 45 angle and containing small holes is positioned in the quartz rod, allowing 1 to 2% of the light to pass into the energy sensor.

===== [p221-221] Alignment > Fine Align: (1块) =====
--[p221]-- In the fine alignment phase all alignment marks are scanned first in x and then in y. Now all specified marks are used for wafer alignment.

===== [p13-13] Wavelength vs. Depth of Focus (2) (1块) =====
--[p13]--  An Image is in focus when the components of the image are re-assembled in the same relationship as when they were disassemble by diffraction at the reticle.
 Depth of Focus refers to the area near the focus plane where the images are near enough to being in focus that the image is usable.

===== [p234-234] Alignment > 4 versus 6 parameter model: (1块) =====
--[p234]-- After alignment has been performed the following wafer parameters are known:
Translation X Translation Y Rotation Expansion (scaling) (Exp) (= 4 parameter modelling) Asymmetric expansion (Y to X scaling) (Exp in x, Exp in y) Non-orthogonality (asymmetric rotation) (total = 6 parameter modelling)
As soon as there are three or more marks aligned (at suitable positions) the system wil use the 6 parameter modelling.
This switch between 4 and 6 parameter modelling is automatic.

===== [p234-234] Alignment > 4 versus 6 Parameter Model (1块) =====

===== [p131-131] Stage Movement Design > Reticle Stage Interferometer Beams Continued (2块) =====
--[p131]-- Thus the reticle stage position in the X,Y plane is referenced to the lens.
This interferometer configuration adequately compensates for any low frequency change in the relative position of the lens top. High frequency vibrations of the lens are dealt with by a feed back loop comprising the stage motors and accelerometers mounted on the lens top.
--[p131]-- During a scanning exposure it is essential to control the position of the scanning stages relative to the projection lens. The top of the lens has some freedom to move with respect to the bottom of the lens, which is fixed to the mainplate.
The interferometers are mounted on the sensor frame for the reticle stage. As illustrated on the slide the reference beam is reflected from the top of the lens (this is the case for all reference beams).

===== [p130-130] Stage Movement Design > Reticle Stage Interferometer Beams (2块) =====
--[p130]-- The configuration of the reticle stage interferometers is illustrated above. The interferometers are used to control the position of the stage in the X,Y plane.
Two beams are used for positioning in the X direction. Each measurement beam is reflected from the stage X mirror twice before reaching the detector. This increases the beam length and consequently the sensitivity of the interferometer.
--[p130]-- This increases the beam length and consequently the sensitivity of the interferometer.
The double reflection also ensures that path to the detector is parallel to the measurement beam path to the X mirror.
As the stage moves only little in X, a full length Y mirror is not required. Therefore corner cubes are used to reflect the Y interferometer beams. These have the advantage that the incident and and reflected rays are parallel.

===== [p213-213] Alignment > Wafer marks: (2块) =====
--[p213]-- The alignment marks on the wafer are called W1, W2,... and so on. Normally, these marks are exposed in the zero layer of production wafers. The marks are exposed with a reticle that contains a 4x wafer mark pattern. After exposure the wafer is developed and the mark pattern will be etched into the silicon. Up to 200 marks may be used on each wafer, but two are normally sufficient for accurate alignment.
--[p213]-- Up to 200 marks may be used on each wafer, but two are normally sufficient for accurate alignment.
A mark typically consists of a pattern of lines and spaces in X and Y for two direction positioning. There are two sets of lines, one having a width of 8.0 µm, the other one has an 8.8 µm width. The reason for this will be explained later on in the presentation.

===== [p68-68] Step & Scan concepts > Step & Repeat principle (1块) =====
--[p68]--  Per step:
– The entire selection of reticle is exposed statically,
– Wafer is moved (stepped) to the next exposure position.
 Three static exposures:
The slide explains the ‘stepping’ principle.
The picture shows light falling on the reticle, partially blocked by the masking blades. The lens then focuses the light on the wafer, which exposes the resist.
In between exposures, the wafer is moved (stepped) to the next exposure position. During exposure all parts of the machine are stationary.

===== [p16-16] Wavelength vs. Depth of Focus (2) > Diffraction Orders captured by Lens (2块) =====
--[p16]--  Focus planes that are conjugate with components of the illumination system are out of focus at the reticle and wafer.
Optical Theory 48142 v00, 12-07-2000 12
--[p16]--  The reticle image diffracts the light into multiple orders. The zero order provides much of the energy required for exposure (refer back to slide 12). The first order of light defines the size and shape of the image.
 The zero order and at least the first orders of light are required to produce an acceptable image on the wafer. The chrome on the reticle (object plane) and the surface of the wafer (image plane) are conjugate planes. (They both are “in focus”)

===== [p220-220] Alignment > Detection of aligned position (2块) =====
--[p220]-- During a scan of the 8.0 m grating of an alignment mark, a sinusoidal signal with an 8.0 m period is detected. The aligned position is defined as the position of the positive top of this signal. However, since there are several tops, the capture range of the system would without further measurements be limited to - 4 m. By means of an extra grating of 8.8 m, the capture range of the alignment system is drastically increased.
--[p220]-- The tops of the alignment signal from this 8.8 m grating are used as a Vernier system to select between tops of the 8.0 m signal as shown in this slide. Coincidence of the 8.0 m and the 8.8 m signals only happens at stage positions that are apart 88 m. (10 x 8.8 = 11 x 8.0 = 88 m ) giving an effective capture range of the vernier system of - 44 m. This fit is done for each X and Y direction.
Alignment 48142 v00, 15-06-00 18

===== [p337-337] Introduction to semiconductor industry FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY (1块) =====
--[p337]-- ASML CONFIDENTIAL
This presentation can be thought of as a general introduction to the semiconductor industry. However, it quickly focuses on those issues that relate to the use of ASML’s lithographic systems, and does not pay as much attention to the other devices that are necessary in a wafer fab (wafer fab = chips production plant). It shows the basic issues that are related to chip manufacturing and then explains the role ASML systems play in the manufacturing process.

===== [p374-374] An Introduction to the PAS 5500 Software FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Start-up/Shutdown (2块) =====
--[p374]-- “Power Off” stops all software and hardware: the system is prepared for power off.
5 - Maintenance: This function “Starts maintenance by bringing machine into an initial safe situation and disabling remote control.”
--[p374]-- There are several start up/shut down options
1 - Start-Up (FAST): Initializes the drivers for any un-initialized subsystems
2 - Start-Up (FULL): Initializes all sub-systems regardless of their current status.
3 - Shut-Down (FULL): Terminates all sub system drivers.
4 - System Shut-Down: There are two sub options, “Power On” stops all scanner software. The system is left ready for a fresh log in. “Power Off” stops all software and hardware: the system is prepared for power off.

===== [p28-29] Determine FPD and AST (Lens Qualification) or BF, Image Tilt and FC (Focal Plane Adjustment) (4块) =====
--[p29]-- This is the fill in screen for computing the lens qualification parameters. After entering the Data File name, the other parameters are extracted from that file. Then you have to decide whether you want to correct for Best Focus, Image Tilt and/or Field Curvature by selecting yes. The field curvature option is only selectable with a dual telecentric lens with moveable elements.
With a scanner and dynamic FOCAL also the other 2 mc’s (reticle stage linear and quadratic wedge) can be corrected for.
--[p28]-- Time needed for readout (247 marks): 247 x 16 x 8 x 3 = 94848 seconds (26 hours)
247 points
16 focus positions
8 wafers
3 seconds for aligning a mark (including stage stepping time between points)
For maintaining the focal plane parameters FOCAL can be done with just 1 wafer and without developing. Then the whole sequence can be done within 25 minutes.
FPD = Focal plane deviation
BF
= Best Focus
AST = Astigmatism
FC
= Field Curvature
--[p28]-- The reason for selecting 13 points for read-out is that the FEM read-out is also done at 13 points. In this way the layout of the image field positions equals those for lens qualification in a FEM. The number of points that are read out is dependent on the reticle layout that is selected in the read-out part of the FOCAL test. It is possible to create some different reticle layouts for the read-out in the Factory constants/Reticle constants/Reticle layout.

===== [p97-97] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > CD absolute grid MCs (2块) =====
--[p97]--  Parameters required in order to define the absolute GRID are:
– Grid Non Orthogonality
– Grid Scaling factor Y to X
– Grid Residuals
 Determined with:
– Non orthogonality and scaling test
– Waferstage mirror unflatness test
– Reticle stage mirror unflatness test
Metrology 48142 v00, 17/01/01 29
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
To get the wafer stage to move in an absolute grid three tests must be performed. The mirror calibration test (wafer and reticle stage) and the non-orthogonality and scaling test.
--[p97]-- The mirror calibration test (wafer and reticle stage) and the non-orthogonality and scaling test.
Together they determine a set of machine constants that define the absolute grid: •mirror residuals (199 points per mirror)
•non orthogonality
•scaling (Y to X scaling factor)

===== [p90-93] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Me5OvlWsTlt (8块) =====
--[p91]-- In each field five marks are exposed, using one mark on the reticle.
This layout is exposed two times:
• once without tilt
• once with different tilts for every mark (see slide), and a shift of 640 micron in Y with respect to the first exposure.
The centre mark is used as a reference: exposed twice without a tilt it is a check on the shift between the two exposures (stage accuracy).
The other marks are used to calculate the impact of tilt on position: the so called Abbe correction. The found correction is stored in machine constants.
--[p90]-- How much the position shifts depends on the rotation point of the stage.
So to make sure that not just the mirror block, but really the fields move in an absolute grid an extra calibration is needed: overlay versus tilt.
This is done by exposing alignment marks on a wafer at different tilt settings of the WS. The wafer is read out, using alignment after which the measured data is modeled. If need be, the machine constants are updated.
The test exposes 43 fields on the wafer.
In each field five marks are exposed, using one mark on the reticle.
--[p92]-- The found correction is stored in machine constants.
The purpose of the test is to determine the Wafer Stage Interferometer MCs. A rotation in the WS results in a shift in the X/Y position of the Fiducial Marks.
This shift is being calibrated for all alignment branch fiducial mark combinations (M1-F1, M1-F2, M2-F1, M2-F2, M1-I0…), by measuring the X and Y shift as function of the rotation of the WS. Which are plotted in the graphical display of the test.
--[p90]--  To calibrate the ‘Abbe correction’:
– tilt causes ‘displacement’ on the wafer
Tilt of the stage is measured by the interferometers. (By comparing upper and lower beams.) This way the position of the mirror block is known.
How ever tilting the mirror block doesn’t only change the position of the field with respect to the focal plane, it also changes it’s X,Y position. How much the position shifts depends on the rotation point of the stage.
--[p92]-- Which are plotted in the graphical display of the test.
This calibration needs to be done, because the HP interferometer beams are not exactly parallel, which results in a different sensitivity for WS rotation/tilts which in turn is machine dependent. After the test is run, a test report is presented with the nominal and aligned mark positions and standard deviations per wafer stage rotation. Large standard deviations might indicate 8 m errors. The fiducial 8.0 to 8.8 m shifts in basic MCs have to be optimized when 8  m errors occur.
--[p93]-- \- Intersection misfit (mm), both x and y interferometer misfit with respect to the blue optical axis of the lens
\- Maximum light path (mm), between lens centre and x and y interferometer.
\- Stage size (mm), physical x and y length of the waferstage
\- Beam length at zero (mm), distances, both x and y, between the intersection point of the interferometer beam to the stage zero position.
--[p92]-- For instance, the center of the WSCS is placed on the center of the wafer CS.
Therefore, all coordinates defined in the WSCS, like the WS Fiducial positions, must be recalculated. (Only for software release 8.3 and below) Due to the limited accuracy with which this can be done, the WS Fiducial and IS plate marks, must be determined again after an update of this test.
\- Interferometer rotation offsets (mrad), both for the X interferometer and Y interferometer block.
\- Interferometer beam distances (mm) between the different interferometer beams and the focal plane
--[p92]-- The fiducial 8.0 to 8.8 m shifts in basic MCs have to be optimized when 8  m errors occur.
8  m errors can also be detected in the graphic presentation. The position displacement in x and y is presented as a function of the stage rotation. When the alignment is OK, the measured curve matches the ideal curve fairly well. When there is a big mismatch like discrete jumps in the curves, an 8  m error has happened.
After this test is done, the WS Coordination System (WSCS) definition is updated. For instance, the center of the WSCS is placed on the center of the wafer CS.

===== [p20-23] Use previous transparency > ASML (10块) =====
--[p22]-- The stepsize is also dependent on the type of process (developed or latent).
A latent FOCAL curve will be wider and therefor the stepsize will be bigger.
XY to Z Conversion
After exposure, PEB and developing, and the measurement of the marks, the XY data has to be converted into Z data. This is the fill in screen for that process. The XY Datafile is the file that is created when the FOCAL wafer was read out at the best energy. The Exposure Focus Values display the focus Offset and the Step size of defocusing.
--[p22]-- When a FEM test is used the MC’s are updated and the focus offset is made zero.
If no FEM test is used and you know there is a focus offset (previous job for instance), this offset has to be added to the focus offset in the FOCAL test. Also the focus can be updated via the FEM fill in screen when you select the “focus only “ option. In focus offset you have to enter the lowest focus value (not the nominal focus value as with FEM!!).
--[p23]-- The Exposure Focus Values display the focus Offset and the Step size of defocusing.
With Graph you can have a look at the FOCAL curves for every mark on every wafer (see examples on next page). With Modify Fit Parameters you have access to the page where you can change the modeling parameters.
--[p22]-- Exposure Focus Offset and Focus Offset Step Developed processes /400 example
/300 offset = - 0.9 µm /400 offset step = 0.12 µm /5x0
/700 offset = -0.75 µm offset step = 0.1 µm
16 Focus levels = 15 steps
FEM reference means that the focus is corrected with a FEM before a FOCAL test is done. This is done only the first time before FOCAL is used. When a FEM test is used the MC’s are updated and the focus offset is made zero.
--[p22]-- The offset and step size values are resist and NA &  dependent.
A certain resist has a bigger DOF than another resist; this means also a bigger FOCAL DOF. If a customer wants to use his own resist to perform FOCAL, the offset and step size have to be determined (by doing FOCAL, trial and error). If the NA is increasing, the DOF is decreasing and this influences the parabolic fit range (will be explained later) of the FOCAL algorithm. The stepsize is also dependent on the type of process (developed or latent).
--[p20]-- This will not be discussed in this presentation.
Once the best exposure dose is known and the process is stable, the single energy option is used to maintain the focal plane parameters.
Normal sequence: 1x determine best exposure dose with FOCAL. Use this exposure dose to perform FOCAL in the future to maintain the focal plane parameters. So an application engineer normally only performs the single energy exposure, except when a new process is started. This means that the process conditions have to be stable.
--[p22]-- Once FOCAL has been done successfully for the first time, FOCAL can be used for maintaining the focal plane and a FEM is not necessary anymore. Note that there is an offset between the focus value of a FEM and FOCAL. This difference can be around 0.1 µm and has to be determined at the customers site. The focus value determined with FOCAL is more accurate than with FEM.
The offset and step size values are resist and NA &  dependent.
--[p20]-- There are 2 different ways to perform FOCAL: With multiple energies or with single energy. The multiple energy option is used to determine the best energy for a specific photoresist and photoresist thickness. This energy is important because it has an influence on the measurement. After the wafer has been exposed and developed, the best energy has to be determined. This will not be discussed in this presentation.
--[p21]-- This involves only 16 exposures (16x1).
For a scanner it’s possible to perform a static or a dynamic exposure. This means that the number of marks that can be read is different for different scanners. For dynamic it is always 247 marks for all scanner types. In static mode it is 39 (13x3) for the DUV scanners (/5x0, /700 and /900) and 91 (13x7) for the i-line scanner (/400).
Exposure Focus Offset and Focus Offset Step Developed processes /400 example
/100 offset = -1.13 µm /2x0 offset step = 0.15 µm /900
--[p21]-- This means that the process conditions have to be stable.
The image on the reticle consisting of 139 marks (stepper layout) or 247 marks (scanner layout) is exposed at 16 different focus heights. When the best exposure dose is already known, the single energy option can be used. This means only 1 exposure dose is used to expose the FOCAL reticle at 16 fields with 16 different focus offsets. This is done at position 1 of the 9 exposure positions as shown in the picture. This involves only 16 exposures (16x1).

===== [p46-46] Compute Machine Status Parameters > Interfield matching: (2块) =====
--[p46]-- Interfield matching is needed to ensure that al fields are exposed on the same locations on the wafer on all machines,
The grid should be the same on all machines.
On the reference machine a reference wafer is made, a first layer with alignment marks. This results in marks located on the wafer in the grid of wafer stage of the reference machine.
--[p46]-- This results in marks located on the wafer in the grid of wafer stage of the reference machine.
With the machine that should be matched, the second layer of marks is printed with an offset of 640 micron, representing the waferstage grid of this machine.
With the alignment system the difference between the mark positions will be measured, after modeling resulting in parameters for:
\- non orthogonality, Y to X scaling
\- local grid residuals
Matching machine constants are available for the interfield distortion terms that can be corrected for the machine that is matched.

===== [p45-45] Compute Machine Status Parameters > Intrafield matching: (2块) =====
--[p45]-- Intrafield matching is done through full field exposures of a metrology reticle that has a matrix of alignment marks.
These marks are printed on a reference wafer by the reference machine. Subsequently the machine to be matched does a second exposure of the same pattern, with a small translational offset (640 micron) with respect to the first layer exposure, on the reference wafer.
The alignment system of the machine is then used to measure the differences between the two patterns.
--[p45]-- The positions of the marks are measured to do this.
After modeling, the differences between the machine and the reference machine (first layer on the reference wafer) are given as intrafield distortion terms. (translation, rotation, magnification and depending on the machine type also third order distortion, asymmetric rotation and asymmetric magnification).
Matching machine constants are available for all intrafield distortion terms that can be corrected for the machine type that is matched.

===== [p6-13] The FOCAL Method > The FOCAL Method (17块) =====
--[p11]-- So the total width of 1 period of the mark is 16 µm (5.8 + 7.2 + 3.0).
The measurement of a FOCAL mark by the alignment system takes place in 2 alignment scans. First the left part of the FOCAL mark is scanned (X and Y), after that the right part, the mirror image, is scanned. With changing focus, the measured center of gravity of the FOCAL mark shifts. This shift is measured by the alignment system and is called alignment offset (AO). The physical position of the marks doesn’t change, only the position measured by the alignment system is different.
--[p11]-- The total alignment offset is the sum of the absolute offsets of the left and right segment of the FOCAL mark (see next page). For 0.4 µm chops, the maximum shift is 3.6 µm per segment, so 7.2 µm is the maximum total AO. The reason we use 2 segments is that we have a bigger alignment offset if we add the two signals. This is important only for very low FOCAL curves.
--[p11]-- For the left and right segment of the mark, the chops are on different sides of the solid line.
With other words the mark consists of two mirrored images. The FOCAL mark is like the primary mark (PM) divided in four segments with both horizontal and vertical lines. The FOCAL mark shown on this page is the one for a /2x0. The pattern of a segment has a wide line (5.8 µm) and 9 chops with a width of 0.4 µm and a ratio of 50%. The space between every set of lines (wide line and chops) is 3.0 µm. So the total width of 1 period of the mark is 16 µm (5.8 + 7.2 + 3.0).
--[p11]-- This is important only for very low FOCAL curves.
Another reason might be that the measurement is not dependent on the absolute aligned position because only the difference is measured. In the graph the total alignment offset of a complete FOCAL mark, so both left & right segment, is displayed. Under the graph only 1 segment, the left one, is shown, going from negative defocus, into focus, to positive defocus.
--[p10]-- As you can see, a good result is reached at the highest dose $( \mathsf { E } _ { 1 } )$
For a correct exposure dose, a bell shaped FOCAL curve is the result. For a lower energy the FOCAL curve is flattening and for too low an energy, the sign of the curve is inverted. If the exposure dose is too high, the curve will get smaller and smaller, until finally there is no usable curve anymore.
--[p8]-- The sine waves as detected with the alignment system shift with respect to the original aligned position when the center of gravity shifts. Note that the actual mark position doesn’t change!!
The principle of FOCAL is that as a function of focus, the measured aligned position will shift with an alignment offset (AO) as result.
The measured AO as a function of focus is put into a graph. This is the so called FOCAL curve.
--[p13]-- 4 The mark exposed at best focus height will have the maximum curve shift. The next curves, as the remainder of the marks are scanned, will have the center of gravity shift again away from the maximum because the mark was exposed further from the best focus height.
Continued on next page
--[p7]-- If the energy used for FOCAL is too low, the chops will not disappear but will get wider.
This could work as well, but because finally the spaces between the chops will close, this will show strange effects.
When the FOCAL mark is in best focus, all lines and spaces, the wide line and the chops are printed properly. The alignment system is used to measure the mark position. The alignment system sees the wide line and the chops as one big line because only first order light can be measured.
--[p6]-- Before we can understand the principle of FOCAL we have to know something about the behavior of the critical dimension (CD) if focus and exposure dose are varied. The critical dimension is the resolved linewidth at wafer level. A Bossung plot is a set of curves of the CD versus focus for several exposure doses. For a positive photoresist, the CD decreases if the exposure dose increases. Depending on this exposure dose the graph shows a top or a bottom while going through the focus range.
--[p13]-- The FOCAL mark has a repeating 16 µm periodic structure which consists of a wide line with several narrow lines, called chops in parallel. Because the chops are small, they will only be resolved when they are at the best focus height. The chops quickly go out of focus when the wafer is moved up or down.
--[p13]-- As is explained already, according to the Bossung plot, the exposure dose is an over-exposure.
2 FOCAL uses the alignment system to measure the FOCAL mark positions.
3 The signal as measured by the alignment system is applied to a curve fit representing the center of gravity or ‘weight’ of the signal. When the mark being sampled was not exposed near the best focus, the chops will not be resolved. Marks that are exposed near best focus will begin to be resolved, resulting in a shift in the center of gravity of the horizontal curve.
--[p7]-- For FOCAL we use a higher than isofocal exposure dose. The reason is that we want to see a change in CD when focus varies. The effect of varying the focus on the CD can be seen on this page. In best focus, all chops are completely resolved. When going out of focus, the chops will become thinner. If we go out of focus even more, the chops will finally disappear. If the energy used for FOCAL is too low, the chops will not disappear but will get wider.
--[p6]-- In between the top- and bottom graphs a graph exists where the linewidth doesn’t change, or changes minimally when the focus changes. In this example it is at 2435 J/m 2 . This exposure dose is called the isofocal point. Ideally this isofocal point is a straight line and this is normally the exposure dose at which exposures takes place, the so called nominal exposure dose.
In case of a higher than isofocal exposure dose, the CD will get smaller and smaller, when going out of focus.
--[p11]-- • Asymmetric alignment marks with chops near the resolution limit of the projection lens
•Chops on /2x0 0.40 µm on /300 0.25 µm on /400 0.40 µm on /5x0 0.25 µm on /700 0.25 µm on /900 0.20 µm
Detail FOCAL mark at wafer level (/2x0)
One FOCAL mark is like a set of 2 OV marks placed very close together. Instead of 1 solid line, the pattern consists of a solid line with chops. For the left and right segment of the mark, the chops are on different sides of the solid line.
--[p9]-- This is the so called FOCAL curve.
The position of the maximum of the graph is representing the focus height at which all chops were printed properly. This is the best focus position.
Here you see the different resulting FOCAL curves when different exposure doses are used. Remember the Bossung plot if you look at this graph. As you can see, a good result is reached at the highest dose $( \mathsf { E } _ { 1 } )$
$$ \mathsf { E } _ { 1 } > \mathsf { E } _ { 2 } > \mathsf { E } _ { 3 } $$
--[p8]-- If the FOCAL mark is going out of focus, the chops will get thinner and thinner until they finally are not printed anymore. This is because the chops are on the operating resolution of the system. The alignment system can then only detect the wide line. The difference between the expected and the measured aligned position, will result in an alignment offset (AO).
--[p13]-- The chops quickly go out of focus when the wafer is moved up or down.
The wide part of the line will continue to be resolved, thus providing a signal from the alignment system, because the 5.8 or 5.5 µm wide part of the line pattern will be resolved throughout most of the vertical range.
1 The FOCAL marks are imaged on the wafer in a pattern of steps, each step having an offset in Z, a fixed distance. In this example each mark was exposed after moving the wafer 0.15 µm. As is explained already, according to the Bossung plot, the exposure dose is an over-exposure.

===== [p26-27] Use previous transparency > FOCAL XY to Z Conversion Report (6块) =====
--[p27]-- The Average St Dev value is the statistical average of the standard deviations (St.Dev).
If it is higher than 80 nm, something is wrong with the measurements and the data shouldn’t be used. Because of tighter specifications this value tends to get smaller (to around 50 nm). In case the standard deviation is out of specification the FOCAL test has to be done again. If only 1 wafer is used, the standard deviation is obviously 0 and no judgment can be made on the accuracy.
The best focus value is determined for every point, both horizontal and vertical on the 4 wafers.
--[p27]-- The best focus value is determined for every point, both horizontal and vertical on the 4 wafers.
With these points the horizontal and vertical focal plane can be determined. By averaging these two you get the best focus average plane. The table with the Best Focii displays the best focus position for horizontal and vertical lines for all measured points. These are the average values of all valid curves measured on the 4 wafers.
--[p26]-- measurements for all wafers.
Valid positions are those for which an acceptable FOCAL result is obtained, using the curve criteria and the wafer reject criterion. The dZ-range is the maximum measured focus position minus the minimum measured focus position.
For example: dz-H = measured best focus position for horizontal lines, tilt is not removed. dz-H-max is the maximum measured best focus position for horizontal lines, tilt is not removed.
--[p26]-- The Best Focus value is the average best focus value of all measured points in horizontal and vertical direction. These measured focus values are shown in the table Best Focii on the next page.
The table with the dZ-ranges displays the number of valid positions per wafer, the dZ-range per wafer and the minimum and maximum dZ values for horizontal and vertical
measurements for all wafers.
--[p26]-- The XY Datafile is the raw datafile from the read out step used for the XY to Z conversion. The Nr of Wafers Used for Calculating Table Best Focii is determined by the number of wafers that were exposed and by the Wafers to be Used for Modeling parameter on the previous page.
The parameters Alignment Offset Lower and Upper, Exposure Focus Values (Offset and Step), Minimum Mcc, Wafer Reject Criterion and Focus Range Parabolic Fit are already explained.
--[p27]-- The table with the Standard Deviations tells us something about the reliability of the measurements. The 5 highest St Dev values displayed here are the 5 highest values of the standard deviation for both horizontal and vertical measurements. Also the mark number where it was measured on (Posit. Nr) is displayed here.
The Average St Dev value is the statistical average of the standard deviations (St.Dev).

--[p25]-- Excludes data points from the modeling when they differ more than this value from the fitted graph.
The wafer reject criterion excludes wafers from the modeling when this percentage or more of the measured points on that wafer have less than the minimum Mcc value. (example: Mcc = 0.4 and wafer reject criterion = 72%. If 72% of the 139 marks have a Mcc value lower than 0.4, the wafer is rejected.)
--[p25]-- The measured alignment offsets (XY data) are converted to focal plane values (Z data). In this fill in screen you have to fill in the parameters for the XY to Z conversion. The XY datafile is the file that is created when the FOCAL wafer was read out at the best exposure dose. The exposure focus values display the focus offset and the step size of defocusing. With the modify fit parameters button you have access to a page where you can change the modeling parameters.
--[p25]-- The alignment offset lower and upper limits are not used.
The model focus range lower and upper limit gives the possibility to exclude data from the modeling. Only data within the focus range will be used for modeling.
The focus range parabolic fit is the range in positive and negative direction around the graph maximum in which the data is modeled.
The minimum Mcc (multiple correlation coefficient) value displays the correlation between the measured points and the fitted graph.

===== [p110-119] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY >  Read out mark positions (7块) =====
--[p110]-- – 12 fields
The wafer is measured by first a global alignment on two marks and then a measurement of all other mark positions. The first layer mark positions are measured and compared to their expected positions. This information is used to calculate the translation X, translation Y, rotation, magnification and 3rd order distortion. This is called first to nominal modeling. The second layer mark positions are measured and compared to the first layer mark positions.
--[p119]-- The image tilt Ry is the same as the static image tilt in Ry and therefor can be measured and updated with the LS tilt Ry MC.
The test determines the MCs: LS height and LS tilt Ry, height of lens elements 1 and 2, reticle stage height, RS linear and quadratic wedge. The system corrects for these last 2 errors by moving the reticle stage up and down on the fly i.e. all actuators move the same amount.
--[p111]-- Expected position
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
The picture on the slide shows an example of two intrafield errors modeled from first to nominal measured mark positions.
--[p110]-- The second layer mark positions are measured and compared to the first layer mark positions.
This information is use to calculate the scan scale and scan skew. This is called second to first modeling.
In order to cancel out WS drifts the global alignment is repeated every 10 minutes. All measurements will be done by the use of the same alignment branch (M1).
The wafer image deviations are fit in a model that includes correctable image deviations. Thus the corrections to the MCs are directly calculated.
Symmetric magnification
Symmetric rotation
Expected position
Actual position
--[p119]-- 91 positions in the field are measured to determine the focal plane (= image plane).
The principle of determining the best focus for each point horizontal and vertical is the same as described in the static FOCAL setup test.
Focus differences between positions in the field (intrafield) are now not only caused by the lens and the level sensor height and tilts, but also by the movement of the reticle stage above the lens. The latter can be defined in the linear and quadratic wedge of the reticle stage.
--[p117]-- – 12 fields
Per field a number of marks (7 x 7) are read. By comparing their positions to the expected ones, we get all (dynamic) intra field parameters. In total 588 marks are read.
This test is exposing the same reticle as the static FOCAL setup test, but instead of only the slit the whole image field is exposed. 91 positions in the field are measured to determine the focal plane (= image plane).
--[p119]-- The latter can be defined in the linear and quadratic wedge of the reticle stage.
These values can be calculated when the average best focus is subtracted from the measured best focus field and a linear plus quadratic fit in the y direction to this corrected field is applied. So the linear RS wedge will correct a dynamically measured image tilt in Y. The image tilt Rx is averaged out and is therefore not included in these calculated values for linear and quadratic wedge.

===== [p105-112] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Me5XySetSkw (8块) =====
--[p106]-- This calibration uses the fact that scan skew and scan scaling are already present in offcenter static exposures. The calibration combines the static red-blue calibration (for redblues and distortion) with the calibration of the stage to stage align parameters skew and scaling.
--[p112]--  First to nominal (zero layer) measured mark positions are
modeled in:
– Intrafield parameters
 Mag, Tx, Ty, Rot and D3
– Corrected by:
 Reticle Height
– E1 and E2
– LS height
 Red blue offsets
--[p105]-- The projection lens has a 4x reduction for (D)UV light
Mark distance on the reticle is 131 mm
Alignment beam distance is not exactly 131 mm
Distance between alignment beam position and real blue image field position is defined by “Red Blue offsets”
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
Me5XySetSkw is often called “static Red-Blue”.
The TTL alignment laser (red) has a wavelength of 633nm
The Illumination light has a wavelength of 248nm or 365nm.
The optical pathlength through the lens is a function of wavelength.
--[p105]-- The redblue offsets give the x, y difference between where the alignment beams actually meet the wafer and where they would be if the system had a 4x reduction for 633 nm light.
--[p105]-- The optical pathlength through the lens is a function of wavelength.
The different wavelengths will take different paths through the optics.
On the reticle the 2 alignment marks are 131 mm away from each other. Because the projections lens has a 4x reduction for 248nm light only, the distance between the alignment beams (633 nm) at wafer level will not be exactly 131/4mm.
--[p105]-- When the system measures the magnification of the reticle, it compares the distance the wafer stage has to move in order to align to both branches with the expected distance of 131mm (at reticle level).
This means that because of the different wavelength, the system will measure a reduction different from 4x and will try to compensate the reticle magnification although it is not necessary
--[p107]--  Static exposure
– zero layer
 Marks A for optical prealignment
 Marks B for global alignment – One branch to be used
The test starts with an exposure of four alignment marks in the zero layer that have maximum distance, marks A for the optical alignment and B for the global alignment. This enforces the wafer alignment to use a single red alignment branch on B. Marks A are used during the optical pre-alignment.
--[p106]-- To ensure an accurate image, and an accurate placement of that image, we need to know the exact position of the red alignment laser beams M1 and M2, with respect to the (D)UV aerial image.
This is determined using an exposure test.
This test calibrates the reticle stage height and Red Blue offsets to minimize the static image distortion. The skew and scaling are also determined, which are compensated for by adapting the expected RS fiducial positions in the MCs.

===== [p48-50] Compute Machine Status Parameters > Matching method (2块) =====
--[p50]-- With ASML matching procedure the BC overlay is optimized as a result.
Matching 48142 v00, 25/08/00 10 Matching 48142 v00, 25/08/00 11 Matching 48142 v00, 25/08/00 12
--[p48]--  AB Matching to improve BC overlay
Matching is based on AB matching as shown on this slide.
One machine is selected as the reference machine, and is then known as reference A. All other machines in the fab, individually known as machine B, are matched to this reference A. This is the AB matching procedure. It is important that the overlay between any of the matched machines, known as BC overlay, be optimized. With ASML matching procedure the BC overlay is optimized as a result.

===== [p80-89] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Sequence: (17块) =====
--[p87]-- In this test, the reticle mark G1 is found manually by the joystick or the spiral search function. The mark is being positioned using the cross hair on the alignment monitor and aligned to one of the wafer stage marks (F2). Which fiducial is used depends on the MC: “Fiducial to be used” that can be found under MCs/ Coordinate System. In today’s scanners, that is in most cases F2 and in old scanners F1. The same alignment procedure is done for the other marks (G2 and H1).
--[p80]-- -Mirror block is globally leveled.
-Mirror block is moved so the IS plate is under the center of the lens
-LVDT values are stored (1)
-IS plate is fine leveled
-LVDT values are stored (2)
-Difference between (1) and (2) is stored as the IS to white LS tilt offset (tilt X and tilt Y)
In this test the height and tilts of F1, F2, RIS (or TIS) plate and the actuator tilt are measured. This is done by first global leveling the mirror block, followed by global leveling of he F1, F2 and IS plate.
--[p81]-- The marks are etched in silicon and are very fragile.
When these marks are damaged due to contamination or (not allowed) cleaning, the plates have to be replaced and the machine has to be re-setup implying other tilts and heights of these plates with respect to the wafer surface.
As a result of this test alignment on fiducial plates can take place without any leveling.
Then the fiducial shifts and the initial Red-Blue offsets are determined by aligning the fiducial mark 1 to the M2 branch.
--[p87]-- The same alignment procedure is done for the other marks (G2 and H1).
It is possible that due to positioning errors of the fiducial plates, the rotation between the RS fiducial plates (dummy reticles with the marks) on the RS, and WS fiducial plates on the WS 8  m errors occur (at wafer level) when a RS mark is aligned. This results in a $3 2 \mu \mathrm { m }$ shift of the RS mark position, moving the mark out of the alignment branch.
--[p82]-- See also the next page.
The RB offsets must be in specifications as stated in the procedure and in check fiducial alignment the measured parameters must be in specifications.

--[p89]-- This test calculates the MC values needed to correct for this unwanted side effect. This is done by measuring the WS rotation at different combinations of translation in X & Y, rotation in Rz and tilt in Rx & Ry settings of the WS, using the 6th interferometer to acquire measurement data.
The X and Y interferometers are 'matched', so they measure the same rotation. This calibration is important when the Athena module is used.
--[p88]-- This test also determines the positions of U1 and U2 on the standard reticle.
This test determines WS IS plate alignment mark I0, I1 and I2 positions as shown.
There are several versions of IS plates that can be mounted on the wafer stage. The alignment mark positions (I0, I1 and I2) on the IS plate, depend on the IS plate type.
Each mark is again found manually or via spiral search in adjustable steps and must be positioned on the cross hair and aligned to the mark on the reticle (K1)
--[p87]-- Then a wafer align i.e. 2 wafer marks (W1, W2) are aligned to 1 reticle mark (K1). At last a stage to stage align is done to get rid of scan scale and scan skew. This Stage to Stage align is performed on the marks G1, G2 and H1 to one wafer stage fiducial (F2). This test determines RS fiducials G1, G2 and H1 positions as used in this global alignment and the RS RIS or TIS marks U1 and U2 positions as shown.
--[p87]-- This is corrected for by visually repositioning the mark on the alignment cross hair and adding the result of that movement to the 8.0 to $8 . 8 \mu \mathrm { m }$ shift values, after which the RS fiducial mark remains correctly positioned. This is repeated including a course determination of the 8.0-8.8 shifts. Then there is a fine tune of these shifts.
This test also determines the positions of U1 and U2 on the standard reticle.
--[p89]-- This calibration is important when the Athena module is used.
The Off Axis module is mounted 65mm from the center of the WS coordinate system. The WS has to travel over this relatively large distance to bring an alignment mark under the OA module. This large travel distance causes a small rotation of the WS since the laser beams are not parallel for both X and Y positioning. The correction of this rotation induces an X, Y translation but by the interferometer model this is corrected.
--[p89]-- Together with procedure Me5WsIfmMc.ADJ and Me5OvlWsTlt.ADJ this procedure forms a set of tests to correct for the translation, rotation and tilt dependencies of the WS interferometer system.
