The fiducial plates F1, F2 and RIS or TIS plates are positioned on the wafer stage by magnets and vacuum. In the test, the relative position (height and tilt) with respect to the E-chuck table top (mirror block) is determined and updated in the MCs.
The marks are etched in silicon and are very fragile.
--[p89]-- This new interferometer model uses some additional MCs next to the existing MCs.
The new MCs in the model are for the new effects called “rotation scaling” and “tilt to rotation cross talk”.
In this test, the wafer stage interferometer model with respect to stage rotations versus translation and tilt is calibrated.
The test takes about 25 minutes and does not use exposures or alignment. When the WS is tilted or translated (moved), a small rotation in Z is also introduced, depending on the amount of tilt or translation in X & Y.

Assuming tilts have been removed and that the RS and WS are parallel in the Z-plane, rotation in SRS and reticle are coarse determined in the Me5CrsSkw.ADJ test, the stages need to be exactly aligned w.r.t to each other in the XY plane. During global align first a reticle align takes place i.e. one mark on the wafer stage (F2) is aligned to 2 marks on the reticle (K1, K2).

===== [p100-102] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Mirror unflatness test (3块) =====
--[p102]-- The wafer is loaded and then read out using alignment.
During readout the stage moves along it’s own mirrors and using it’s own interferometer. Therefore any deviation between the current wafer stage grid and the ‘absolute” wafer grid will be measured. These deviations will be seen as differences between expected and measured position of the marks.
Instead of just one mark per position, there are actually groups of marks on the wafer. (See slide) The marks are rotated 90° with respect to each other.
--[p102]-- (See slide) The marks are rotated 90° with respect to each other.
This special lay out is used to create an ortho wafer. The wafer is exposed in the normal orientation. After development the wafer is rotated 90° and read out on the same machine. In this way the non-orthogonality and the Y to X scaling can be determined and corrected for.
It is very important to use the SAME wafer for the absolute grid calibrations on all the machines in a fab.
--[p100]-- This test is very similar to the one for the waferstage. The difference is that only the Xmirror needs to be measured.
For the reticle stage a total of 151 residuals are measured for X.
A pre-qualified wafer is used: the “ortho wafer”. This etched wafer has a cross of PM marks on it. The cross is “perfect”: perpendicular and equal distances between the marks. (Perfect = within a certain specification.)
The wafer is loaded and then read out using alignment.

===== [p78-80] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Note: (7块) =====
--[p80]-- During an IS scan the E-chuck is globally leveled and the IS plate is field by field leveled.
The machine knows how much the IS plate must be tilted in order to go from the globally leveled E-chuck plane to the field by field leveled IS plane (defined by the IS to white LS tilt offset). If a different tilt is measured, and it is assumed that the LS tilt did not drift, the measured tilt error (difference between old and new tilt) equals the stone to lens tilt. The offset value is calculated and the stone to lens tilt drift MC will be corrected with this value.
--[p78]-- In this test the same part of the reticle (slit position 2) will be exposed at 3 different locations on the wafer, although the fill in screen in the PAS-software is suggesting differently!
This test is used to calibrate the stone to lens tilt with respect to the level sensor tilt. Level sensor tilt must be known and valid. Stone to lens tilt represents the angle of the stone with respect to the focal plane of the lens.
--[p79]-- This analysis can be done using the LCPLOT tool that uses the LC diagnostic files. More detailed information can be found in the apps note EDL (edge die leveling): Stone to lens tilt determination.
If a global level on the mirror block is performed followed by a field by field leveling of the IS plate, you measure a certain tilt difference. This tilt difference is known as the image sensor to white level sensor tilt offset (IS to white LS tilt offset).
--[p80]-- In this test this IS to white LS tilt offset is determined and stored in the MCs.
Two WS chucks are shown with the locations of the RIS and TIS plate locations. Also the locations of the marks on the plates are shown.
During an IS scan the E-chuck is globally leveled and the IS plate is field by field leveled.
--[p79]-- Stone to lens tilt represents the angle of the stone with respect to the focal plane of the lens.
Level Sensor tilt represents the angle of the level sensor measurement plane with respect to the focal plane of the lens. The stone to lens tilt is needed for 2 reasons:
1. During global leveling to bring the wafer in the focal plane (I.e. in the capture range of the white level sensor system).
2. In the extrapolation of the height for edge dies measured in None\_By\_LS mode (i.e. no level sensor measurement at all) as shown on the slide.
--[p79]-- The average actuator tilt as measured equals the tilt of the stone with respect to the stage.
This average actuator tilt plus the fitted tilt represent the stone to level sensor tilt. Assuming that the level sensor tilt is known, the test can determine the stone to lens tilt.
When CD variation at the edges of a wafer (None\_by\_LS fields) occurs it might be caused by a improper Stone to Lens tilt calibration. An indication for the error in the Stone to lens tilt MCs can be found when there is a difference between the tilt of the fitted Z plane and the average actuator tilt value.
--[p79]-- In the test (valid for 8.3 & up), the wafer is leveled in All\_by\_LS mode at equally distributed locations on the wafer. The raw actuator values are corrected for Die Under Exposure (DUE) i.e. the height correction due to tilting the wafer stage. A plane is fitted through these corrected values and corresponds to the total wedge of the wafer and the chuck. The average actuator tilt as measured equals the tilt of the stone with respect to the stage.

===== [p96-96] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Me5WafMc (5块) =====
--[p96]-- This is the static wafer offset.
The wafer will be loaded in the wrong Y position and when the wafer stage is back under interferometer control, the wafer stage coordinate system in Y is adjusted.
The calibration wafer is loaded with use of the mark sensor. The loading error is determined using the alignment system. Translation X and rotation are corrected for adjusting the wafer stage load position. Translation Y is corrected for adjusting the static wafer offset (SWO). The maximum value of the SWO is limited to 100 µm.
--[p96]-- A translation in X and a rotation can be corrected for by adjusting the wafer stage load position.
For X and rotation, the wafer stage is still under interferometer control in the load position. A translation in Y can not be corrected for in the same way. If the wafer stage is in the load position, it is not under interferometer control in the Y direction. So another correction has to be applied to correct for a translation Y loading error. This is the static wafer offset.
--[p96]-- The maximum value of the SWO is limited to 100 µm.
If the loading error in Y would become bigger, the wafer would be loaded to much off centered with respect to the E-table. If the test measures a SWO that is bigger, a hardware adjustment is necessary to adjust the position of the load sensor. This is usually already done in the factory and should not be necessary in the field.
As a result of this test the report will show values for wafer stage load position MCs for X and Rz. And the static wafer offset for Y.
--[p96]-- Determine the wafer mark (W1 and W2) 8.0 to 8.8 shifts, Red Blue offsets and adjust the wafer load position (wafer stage load position and static wafer offsets).
From a calibration wafer with printed PM marks the positions W1 and W2 and 8.0 to 8.8 shifts are measured using the red alignment system. If the wafer is not loaded on the correct position on the E-chuck, this can be corrected for. A translation in X and a rotation can be corrected for by adjusting the wafer stage load position.
--[p96]-- Determine the wafer mark (W1 and W2) 8.0 to 8.8 shifts, the Red Blue offsets and adjust the wafer load position.
 From a calibration wafer with PM marks the positions W1 and W2 are measured.
– This ensures that the wafer is loaded exactly in the correct position using the load sensor.
 Adjustment of the offset is limited to 100 micron in Y,
– or a hardware adjustment needs to be performed.
– usually already done in the factory and should not be necessary in the field.
ASML CONFIDENTIAL

===== [p2-2] FOCAL = FOcus Calibration using ALignment > – Key Benefits (3块) =====
--[p2]-- The measurement is done with the alignment system.
Because we can measure on a lot of points in the image field, we can determine the complete focal plane. FOCAL is a very fast and accurate method with almost no human influence. The stepper or scanner is used for the measurement and this is both an advantage as an disadvantage. An advantage because there is no human influence and it is accurate. A disadvantage because the exposure tool is used for measuring instead of production.
--[p2]-- A disadvantage because the exposure tool is used for measuring instead of production.
The measured data is used directly in the stepper software so this decreases the chance of errors in the interpretation.
--[p2]-- • Fast & accurate
• Automatic, human independent
• Exposure tool measures
• Data is directly used by the PAS software
• Possibility of determining the complete focal plane
48142 v00, 19/10/99
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
For a stepper or scanner it is important to measure the best focus position on a regular basis. With FOCAL this best focus position can be measured in a different way as the optical or SEM readout of a FEM. The measurement is done with the alignment system.

===== [p103-104] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Me5IqFoc (9块) =====
--[p104]-- From these best focus values for horizontal and vertical of all positions of the image field the focus offset, the image tilt in X and Y (=LS height and tilt in X and Y) and the field curvature can be determined. The height of the lens elements E1, E2 and reticle stage (E0) are to correct for field curvature. Ls height and tilt MCs are changed to correct for focus and tilt errors.
--[p103]-- A certain number of FOCAL marks are distributed over the image field.
The more FOCAL marks, the more accurately the focal plane will be determined. In total 16 fields are exposed on the wafer, each with an increased focus height. One field is exposed at nominal focus (expected to be best focus). When the best exposure dose is already known, the single energy option can be used. This means only 1 exposure dose is used to expose the FOCAL reticle at 16 fields with 16 different focus offsets.
--[p103]-- This alignment offset plotted against the defocus will result in a parabolic curve.
The focus height that provides the maximum AO is the best focus position of this location in the field.
--[p104]-- Therefor, at customer site, the FOCAL process must be optimized to customer resists.
This is described in detail in the application note “PAS 5500 FOCAL Photoresist implementation”
For a scanner it’s possible to perform a static or a dynamic exposure. This means that the number of marks that can be read is different for different scanners. For dynamic it is always 247 marks for all scanner types. In static mode it is 39 (13x3) for the DUV scanners (/5x0, /700 and /900) and 91 (13x7) for the i-line scanner (/400).
--[p103]-- To calibrate the static focal plane, using the FOCAL method. The FOCAL method (or better: the modeling) strives towards finding the best possible projected image, or focal plane. The test updates the LS height, tilts (Rx and Ry), the RS Height and the height of lens elements E1 and E2. The FOCAL method uses the alignment system in combination with chopped marks to determine the focal plane. A certain number of FOCAL marks are distributed over the image field.
--[p104]-- Ls height and tilt MCs are changed to correct for focus and tilt errors.
On a scanner, first a static FOCAL is performed with a small image field (as large as the slit size which is determined by the quartz rod) and then after the static XY, a dynamic FOCAL with a large image field.
The determination of static and dynamic FOCAL is performed on ASML standard resists that in most cases are not available at customer site. Therefor, at customer site, the FOCAL process must be optimized to customer resists.
--[p104]-- The PAS software determines this best focus by fitting a parabolic curve through the measured points. It depends on the quality of the FOCAL process if the results will be accurate. A good FOCAL curve must have a large alignment offset and six points in the parabola.
This whole procedure is done for horizontal and vertical lines resulting in two curves. The astigmatism at this point in the image field is determined by looking at the difference in best focus between horizontal and vertical lines.
--[p103]-- This is done at position 1 of the 9 exposure positions as shown in the picture. This involves only 16 exposures (16x1).
The alignment system is used after exposure and development to determine the best focus of this FOCAL mark and so the best focus of this position in the image On the next page a cross-section of the FOCAL mark is shown for different focus steps.
--[p103]-- The chops near the wider line decrease in width when going out of focus, when a higher energy than best energy is used (remember Bossung plots). The arrows above the chops indicate where the alignment system will align on these marks. The aligned position will shift if the chops are present in best focus, resulting in a maximum alignment offset. This alignment offset plotted against the defocus will result in a parabolic curve.

===== [p24-24] Use previous transparency > XY to Z Conversion FOCAL Curves (1块) =====

===== [p4-4] FOCAL = FOcus Calibration using ALignment > Can be used on steppers and scanners (2块) =====
--[p4]-- • UV:
(/100), /2x0 and /400
• DUV: /300, /5x0, /700 and /900
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
Currently FOCAL is mainly used for lens qualification and for determining and maintaining the focal plane parameters. These parameters are best focus, image tilt X and Y and field curvature (FC). The FC is correctable only on systems with a dual telecentric lens. On scanners also the reticle stage vertical constants can be calibrated to optimize the reticle stage linear and quadratic wedge.
--[p4]-- FOCAL can also be used to fine calibrate the lens heating parameters. This process is called FOCAL Fine Tune (FFT).
FOCAL is frequently used on the /2x0, /300, /400, /5x0 and /900 for maintaining the focal plane parameters (LS setup) and for lens qualification (LQ). FOCAL is being implemented on the /100 system as well. At the moment FOCAL can be used for maintaining the focal plane parameters on a /100 but not yet for lens qualification.
In the future FOCAL can also be used on the /22, /60 and /80. This is currently being investigated by Special Applications (SA).

===== [p19-19] Use previous transparency > ASML CONFIDENTIAL (1块) =====
--[p19]-- The machine constants that are being updated are dependent on the type of system and the type of test that is being executed. For a single telecentric lens the level sensor height and tilt mc’s are updated. For a double telecentric lens, also the reticle table height and the height of lens elements 1 and 2 are updated, For a scanner it’s possible to update the reticle stage linear and quadratic wedge. These last 2 mc’s can only be updated if you perform a dynamic FOCAL test.

===== [p44-44] Compute Machine Status Parameters > Matching versus calibration (3块) =====
--[p44]-- Since the absolute grid is used on scanners (since 1999) there is no need to calibrate interfield matching parameters any longer.
When a machine to machine matching calibration is less accurate than the individual machine calibrations, some parts of the matching calibration may not be necessary.
--[p44]-- All of the calibrations are based on measurements that are done with the machine’s internal metrology systems (alignment, interferometers, level sensor, image sensor etc.) Because of differences between these systems from machine to machine, both grid and distortion calibrations are not fully accurate. Resulting in machine to machine overlay errors.
The distortion calibrations can be found with increased precision if machine matching is done as one more calibration.
--[p44]--  All calibrations based on machines internal metrology systems
Distortion calibration with increased precision if machine matching is done as one more calibration.
ASML CONFIDENTIAL
Each machine is equipped with metrology software that permits the calibration of the waferstage grid and the lens distortion.
The precision with which the calibration parameters can be determined may change from machine to machine type and software version.

===== [p94-95] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Me5StoMap (5块) =====
--[p94]-- This is measured by the interferometer system.
The stone to lens tilt (as previously determined) compensates for the stone not being parallel to the focal plane and the stone map compensates for the stone not being flat. Both contribute to an optimal edge die leveling performance. Each time the stone map is measured, the current stone map is zeroed. Therefore, a verification of the measurement by repeating the test is not possible.
--[p94]-- Therefore, a verification of the measurement by repeating the test is not possible.
As previously described, the interferometer system can determine the tilt Rx, Ry (with an accuracy better than 0.5 microrad) of the mirrorblock by the optical path difference of the top and bottom laser beams X1 and X2 of the interferometer system. Since the chuck is fixed to the airfoot, Ry or Rx is a measure of the local stone tilt and the local mirror tilt.
--[p94]-- The Z map of the stone is calculated by the integration of the tilts in X and Y since these are the derivative of the height Z in x and y. During measurement of the tilt map, SWS actuators are fixed in height Z and tilts Rx and Ry, while stepping over the stone in a predefined grid (is adjustable).
At the end a fourth order polynomial will be fitted through the stone heights and the constants in the polynomial will be stored as MCs.
--[p94]--  determines height and tilt map of the SWS stone.
 measured with the interferometer system.
contributes to an optimal edge die performance.
– stone to lens tilt compensates for the stone not being parallel to the focal plane,
– the stone map compensates for the stone not being flat.
In this test the height and tilt map of the SWS stone is determined. This is measured by the interferometer system.
--[p95]-- Except for the fixed offset and the linear values (stone to lens tilt) of the polynomial since these are already determined by the tilt matching test.
The stone profiles can be viewed in the PAS software for every x or y in a cross-sectional modeled view as shown.
Also the fit residuals of these profiles can be viewed. A more detailed description of this test can be found in the application note: “Stone Map”

===== [p115-116] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Me5XySetDyn (3块) =====
--[p115]-- The test works the same as the static test with the difference that whole scan fields instead of only the slit are exposed. In total 20 fields are exposed (overlay job), though only 12 fields are read out.
--[p115]--  Dynamic XY-calibration
– Set\_up\_matching\_SC reticle
– 13 x 19 = 247 marks per field
This test minimizes the dynamic image distortion by calibrating the intrafield parameters of CD MCs: Translation X,Y, Rotation, Magnification, 3rd order distortion, Scan Skew and Scan Scaling. These CD corrections are offsets with respect to the IQ MCs as determined by the static XY plane set up test.
--[p116]--  Scanning exposure
– one layer (aligned to a zero layer)
– 20 fields
– 4940 marks total
One layer of full fields is exposed (so a scanning exposure).

===== [p121-121] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY >  Matching layouts (2块) =====
--[p121]-- Depending on the systems that need to be matched the layout is different.
Main reasons: step&scan has a bigger field size and we don’t match the interfield parameters on step&scan systems.
By doing second to first modelling, the differences between the intra fields of the two scanners can be measured, and stored in a separate set of machine constants.
--[p121]-- Because of different machine types and different matching requirements, there are three different layouts possible.
Stepper mirror map layout
Scanner mirror map layout
Scanner overlay layout
The layout consists of a lot of marks, full field exposures of the overlay reticle for the intra field (steppers and scanners), crossbars exposures for the inter field (steppers only).
Depending on the systems that need to be matched the layout is different.

===== [p98-98] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Me5WsMirFla (3块) =====
--[p98]-- Beam C will control the X-position and with beam D the X-position is measured.
The wafer stage is stepped in Y, while beam D measures the behaviour of the X-mirror. Measurements are taken every 0.5 mm. At the end of the mirror the sequence is repeated, but beams C and D change functionality. So beam D controls the X-position, while beam C measures the Xposition.
Then the whole sequence is repeated for the other mirror. So beam C is used to control Xposition, beam D to control rotation. Beam B is used to control the Y-position and beam A is used to measure the Y-position.
--[p98]--  Beam A determines step in Y
 Beam B will be used to keep Rz constant
For this test no exposures are needed. The interferometer system has two beams in the X-direction and two beams in the Y-direction. This means rotation can be measured on both mirrors.
Beam A is used to control the Y-position, beam B is used to control the rotation. Beam C will control the X-position and with beam D the X-position is measured.
--[p98]-- Beam B is used to control the Y-position and beam A is used to measure the Y-position.
The wafer stage is stepped in X, while beam A measures the behaviour of the Y-mirror. At the end of the mirror the sequence is repeated with changed beam A and B functionality.
The results are stored in the MCs as 199 residuals for X, Y Rx and Ry.

===== [p120-120] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > – Intra-Field: (2块) =====
--[p120]-- The reason not to match the inter field is the fact that exposures are done ‘scanning’.
Changing the way the wafer stage moves, would cause a ‘miss-match’ between scanning reticle stage and scanning wafer stage. This could result is very strange intra field exposures. On top of that, all stage grids have been calibrated according to an absolute grid already. So in principle the interfield behaviour of all systems should already be the same.
--[p120]--  Magnification
 Rotation
 Translation
 3rd order distortion
Asymmetric rotation
 Asymmetric magnification
– Determined with intrafield matching test
The matching test is designed to make two (or more) machines move and expose in the same way. This is done by comparing the intrafield behaviour of two machines.
On step&scan systems only the intra field is matched. The reason not to match the inter field is the fact that exposures are done ‘scanning’.

===== [p31-33] Compute Machine Status Parameters (4块) =====
--[p33]-- This is the fill in screen for the calculation of the focal plane adjustment parameters.
Here you can enter which adjustment parameters should be calculated: Best Focus, Image Tilt and/or Field Curvature. Field curvature is correctable only on a system with the two moveable projection lens elements (/300, /400 and /5x0). With a scanner (/400 and /5x0) and dynamic FOCAL also the other 2 mc’s (reticle stage linear and quadratic wedge) can be corrected for.
The reticle layout determines how many points are read-out on the FOCAL wafer, so how many points are used for modeling.
Normally 13 points are selected because this is identical to the FEM test.
--[p31]-- The ATP specification is given on the next page.
The FPD (Focal Plane Deviation) is the difference between the maximum best focus position and the minimum best focus position of either horizontal or vertical lines, after tilt and offsets have been corrected for. This parameter is used for steppers.
The IPD (Image Plane Deviation) is a similar parameter as the FPD used for scanners. Because of the scan in the Y-direction, the FPD in the Y-direction is averaged out.
--[p31]-- Because of the scan in the Y-direction, the FPD in the Y-direction is averaged out.
The astigmatism is the absolute value of the maximum difference between best focus of horizontal and vertical lines. In the astigmatism graph the difference H-V is displayed, so this can also be a negative value. The specification for astigmatism is always a positive value however, because it is expressed as:
| BFH - BFV |.
This is the fill in screen for the calculation of the focal plane adjustment parameters.
--[p31]-- FPD= 0.24 µm AST= 0.13 µm
These 2 graphs are not from the result from the PAS software. They show the focal plane and the astigmatism of a lens measured with FOCAL. Note that this is not the same data as in the other reports of the 4062 machine. From the focal plane graph the focal plane deviation (FPD) can be calculated. The ATP specification is given on the next page.

===== [p47-47] Compute Machine Status Parameters > Absolute grid: (1块) =====
--[p47]-- When both the reticle and wafer stages move absolutely parallel with respect to each other, and in a perfect straight line, we have an absolute grid. All step and scan machines, when properly calibrated, have an absolute grid.

===== [p77-77] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > Method: (2块) =====
--[p77]-- The height and the tilt are calculated for each field.
The average of the 3 height and tilt measurements is then used to update the MCs.
Note: there is no red system height measurement in each field after white level. This is because the slit area is small, so wafer unflatness has little effect.
--[p77]-- -The wafer is globally leveled (red and white)
-The first field is white leveled (All\_by\_LS)
This brings the wafer surface level with the level sensor plate
-The field matrix is exposed (31 steps), stepping the wafer in Z (so to different heights)
-The sequence is then repeated for the other two fields.
\- The best focus of the 4 corner position in each field is then read under the microscope. The height and the tilt are calculated for each field.

===== [p108-108] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY >  Static exposure (1块) =====
--[p108]-- – first layer
 aligned to the zero layer
 12 fields
 468 marks total
Then 12 fields are statically exposed using the SETUP\_MATCHING\_SC reticle at the reticle stage zero position.
Between exposures a global alignment is done to average out alignment errors.

– For the /400, /5x0, /700 and /900 the reticle layout is similar to the Overlay reticle layout with FOCAL marks instead of XPAmarks, total 247 marks
The layout is similar to that of the overlay reticles. The only difference is that the mark positions are different.

===== [p109-109] FOR TRAINING PURPOSES ONLY > – second layer (2块) =====
--[p109]--  12 fields
 468 marks total
640 micron shift in Y with respect to the first layer
 from six different parts of the reticle
On top of this (in the same exposure test), a second layer with six static images from different Y-positions of the reticle is exposed on the wafer with a shift of 640  m in Y between the two layers. This way the reticle stage has to move as if it was scanning.
Each of those images will suffer from a placement error caused by scan skew and scan scaling.
--[p109]-- Each of those images will suffer from a placement error caused by scan skew and scan scaling.
This results in a displacement not equal to 640 µm in Y and 0 in X between the two layers.

===== [p3-3] FOCAL = FOcus Calibration using ALignment > –Drawbacks (2块) =====
--[p3]-- •Reproducibility depends on photoresist process
•Readout is time consuming, exposure tool needed
•Limited capture range
•Photoresist specific best focus determination
•System must be setup for exposures:
•illumination uniformity
•Rema
•coarse focus and tilt
Despite all the benefits there are also some drawbacks. They are listed above. The limited capture range will be explained further on in the document.
--[p3]-- The limited capture range will be explained further on in the document.
The photoresist specific best focus determination, is not only an disadvantage for FOCAL, but for all best focus processes that use a photoresist (like the FEM).

===== [p5-5] The FOCAL Method (1块) =====
--[p5]-- Fundament is asymmetrical mark design:
FOCAL MARK: chopped portion, 0.40 or 0.25 µm l/s
This is the basic layout of the FOCAL mark. As you can see it consists of a solid line and so called chops. The first FOCAL mark was made based on a PM mark. A part of the 8 µm wide line of the mark was chopped into smaller lines. That explains where the name ‘chops’ comes from. Later a number of chops was added in the space area for reasons of a bigger capture range.

===== [p42-42] Compute Machine Status Parameters > Matching: (1块) =====
--[p42]-- The use of the matching strategy will let all machines in a fab be matched to a reference machine.
The overlay is optimized as a result. If an exposure of one product layer is done on one machine, an exposure of a subsequent layer for that product can be done on any other machine and the overlay for these layers is optimized.

===== [p45-45] Compute Machine Status Parameters > Basic idea of matching (1块) =====
--[p45]--  Expose the same layer-layout twice
 On two different machines
 Second layer has a shift with respect to the first layer
 Measure positions using alignment
Differences between expected and measured positions are used to calculate new parameters and produce a set of matching machine constants
